# KCore's coverage of K, generated from the construct table

Generated by `test/constructs/coverage.py` from `test/constructs/rows.toml` and `../core-manifest.toml` (paths from `kcore-spec`); do not edit it. `python3 -I test/constructs/coverage.py` writes it, and `test/constructs.sh` fails (through `coverage.py check`) when it differs from what the two files give. Every status, class and count below is read from `rows.toml`; `rows-header.toml` says what each field means, and `designs/DONE-0-definition-of-done.md` C1 to C3 what the table must hold.

Statuses (DONE-0 C2): **supported** (read, the kernel gives it the meaning named), **parameter** (numbered, uninterpreted; the theorems hold for every interpretation H), **inert** (no meaning in the semantics, with the reason), **erased** (kompile leaves no trace in `definition.kore`), **refused** (the reader, or kompile, stops with a named reason). Two more mark rows not yet decided: **pending-fixture** (the decision waits for a fixture) and **not-handled** (no recorded place gives a status); neither is a status at the release.

Refusal classes (DONE-0 C2, rows-header.toml): **R-a** outside the fragment by a recorded decision; **R-b** the option value or text form is outside the language K's documentation or the KORE syntax document states; **R-c** the kernel has no notion for it, naming the cone declaration that would change; **scope** nothing excludes the form, but no text in scope holds it.

Cells are cut at 300 characters (a case's text at 120) and end in "…" where cut; `rows.toml` holds each field whole. A nested entry (a row's `cases`, `also_refused` or `split`, as `test/constructs/units.py` walks them) is the line after its row, named by its place.

## Rows not yet decided (5)

- E2 `INDEX(index)@frontend`: pending-fixture; pending: No fixture (\`fixture = "none"\`, 'not tried'). Expected erased; C2 wants the pair.
- E2 `INITIAL(initial)@frontend`: pending-fixture; pending: No fixture. A cell property read by GenerateSentencesFromConfigDecl (:778, :788): it may change initializer equations; not erased until a pair shows it.
- E2 `ML_BINDER(mlBinder)@frontend`: pending-fixture; pending: The key marks kast.md's #Exists and #Forall productions; its effect is \`\\exists\`, \`\\forall\` in rule bodies, which are E3 rows. The E2 row should take the E3 rows' statuses by rule S1, not one of its own. The e0 fixtures exist (e3-ml-rule-\*, e3-ml-eq-\*, e3-ml-req-\*, e3-ml-lhs-empty-sort; the E3 rows'…
- E2 `ML_OP(mlOp)@frontend`: pending-fixture; pending: As ML\_BINDER, for #Ceil, #Equals and the other matching-logic productions (E3). The e0 fixtures exist (e3-ml-rule-\*, e3-ml-eq-\*, e3-ml-req-\*, e3-ml-lhs-empty-sort; the E3 rows' cases give their statuses by position); TRIAGE-2 decides this row.
- E2 `PARSER(parser)@frontend`: pending-fixture; pending: No fixture. Expected erased (a program-parsing key, class \`use\`).

## What the cone teaches

The layers of `core-manifest.toml` in their order, each with its `teaches` field (DONE-0 C11: the release gate and the completion gate read each against the cone's definitions) and the declarations it names; then the `except` entries and what the manifest puts outside the core.

| order | layer | status | teaches | declarations |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `terms` | stable | what a configuration is: ground terms, patterns with variables, sorts and symbols as numbers | KCore.GroundTerm, KCore.Term, Srt, Sym, KCore.Subst |
| 2 | `signature` | stable | a definition as a Lean value: sorts, constructors, subsorts, functions as clauses with priorities, rules with priorities, collections, overloading symbols (a symbol at larger sorts that agrees with constructors at smaller sorts on injected arguments) | KCore.Semantics, KCore.CtorSig (KcoreSpec/FixedSignature.lean), KCore.Fn, KCore.Clause, KCore.Rule, KCore.CollSig, KCore.CollKind, KCore.OvlSig |
| 3 | `values` | stable | which ground terms are values of which sort; configurations as well-sorted terms of the top sort | sortOf, sortsOf, KCore.Value, KCore.Config, WellSorted |
| 4 | `matching` | stable | when a pattern matches a value under a substitution, including map, list and set patterns (a set pattern that concatenates matches a disjoint split) and a pattern headed by an overloading symbol, as a relation | KCore.PatVal, KCore.PatVals |
| 5 | `evaluation` | stable | function evaluation as a relation: a clause decides where it applies and every clause of a smaller priority fails; no result where every clause fails; the order of clauses has no meaning | KCore.Eval, KCore.EvalTerm, KCore.EvalTerms, KCore.Fails, KCore.ReqFails |
| 6 | `step` | stable | one step: a rule whose left side matches, whose requires is true, whose right side has a value, and no rule of a smaller priority applies | KCore.Step, KCore.NotApplies, what Step.lean reads of rules: requires and priority |
| 7 | `hypothesis` | stable | what the theorems assume of a definition's functions, as four statements about evaluation: every function has a result on well-sorted arguments; every [total] function has a value; two clauses of one priority agree where both apply; two matches of one clause agree | KCore.FnHyp, KCore.Coherent, KCore.MatchIndependent, KCore.ClausesAgree |
| 8 | `matching-logic` | stable | patterns, models and Γ ⊧ φ of applicative matching logic, as much as the statements use | ML.Pattern, ML.Interpretation, the entailment Γ ⊧ φ, definedness, KoreAML.sNext, KoreAML.allNext |
| 9 | `theory` | stable | the theory Γ a definition denotes, built from the Lean value: sorts and subsorts, constructors (no confusion, no junk), collections (four map axioms, five list axioms, four set axioms, as for maps, with concatenation defined on disjoint sets), overloading symbols (each overload as an equation, and distinctness of uncovered applications), function equations in priority form with the closure axiom, rule axioms; and the denotation of a configuration | KCore.Faithful.Γ, KCore.Faithful.Γstep, KCore.den (KcoreSpec/Statements.lean), ΓSig, ΓCons, ΓColls, ΓFns, ΓRules |
| 10 | `statements` | stable | SoundC: Γ with the step closure has a model and every step is entailed. CompleteC: every successor the theory forces is a step. The two theorems, with their two hypotheses | KCore.SoundC, KCore.CompleteC, KCore.StepIs, KCore.StepFn, KCore.sound\_hyp, KCore.complete\_hyp |
| 11 | `well-formedness` | stable | coreOK, the decidable check on the semantics, as six conjuncts, each in words with its Lean text. sortedOK: subsorts a strict partial order whose upper sorts are user sorts or library constructor sorts, every clause and every rule well sorted; a token sort of the definition has its literals as values and no constructors. faithCoreOK: twelve naming and numbering conditions, each closing a way the theory Γ would otherwise have no model. hooksApart, predsApart, idsOK: names apart and numbering. overloadsOK: each overloading symbol is declared once, plainly named, over constructors or earlier overloading symbols, with coherent overloads. What coreOK does not ask, because no proof of either theorem reads it: that every sort has a value, that signatures are over declared sorts, that a map pattern has one frame, that a function without clauses is never applied | KCore.Semantics.coreOK, sortedOK, subsortsOK, libConsSorts, clauseSorted, ruleSorted, faithCoreOK, hooksApart, predsApart, idsOK, overloadsOK |

Exceptions inside the layers:

| layer | declaration | status |
|---|---|---|
| `signature` | KCore.Lib, and the registry in KcoreSpec/FixedSignature.lean (everything but Srt, Sym and CtorSig), BuiltinNames.lean, BuiltinData.lean, Builtins.lean | stable |
| `signature` | KCore.Semantics.hooks, KCore.HookParams, KCore.ParamSig | stable |
| `signature` | KCore.Semantics.params | stable |
| `evaluation` | silentTable, silent, Semantics.fallback, Semantics.hookRes (KcoreSpec/Hooks.lean); Faithful.ΓFallback | stable |
| `evaluation` | KcoreSpec/Hooks.lean (the Bool, Int and Float operations, HookParams) | stable |
| `evaluation` | strictness of Bool hooks | stable |
| `theory` | ΓDomains | stable |
| `well-formedness` | inside overloadsOK: an overloading symbol is not named like ite at a sort | stable |
| `well-formedness` | inside faithCoreOK: the first half of itesApart (every symbol named like ite is named at a numbered sort) | provisional |

Not core:

- every proof
- the reader (Parse) and the tie to the KORE text: trusted only when a definition is read without the tie
- the generator and every generated package
- the test system: test/integration.sh per merged batch (pins of the statements, axioms of every declaration, cone diff, witnesses, corpus, round trip, agreement with reference backends on cases) and test/release.sh (every example package, KEVM, mir)
- the deciders and the executable evaluators (the one for collection patterns is proved to list exactly Step's successors: KCore.CExec.step\_iff)
- theorem kinds (invariants, determinism, noninterference), certificates, the symbolic layer: taught later as uses of the core, each when its interface is stable
